29{tshogs chos lung pa'i mkhan po mdzad pas mkhas chen zhes zhu zhing/mtshan dngos grags pa gzhon nu yin/lha sa'i shar phyogs dbus stod kyi lha lcang ri mo can zhes pa'i grong gyang gsar du rab byung zhus/mkhan chen pa'i drung nas (phar phyin )dang (spyod 'jug )blo sbyong )sogs mdo sngags kyi chos mang po gsan/zul phu'i mkhan ches chos grags rin chen dang/khro phu'i ba dpon gzhon nu mdzes pa/mkhan 29{rje 'di ni rgyal ba mnyam med chen po shes rab rgyal mtshan gyi khri 'dzin mkhan rogs nyer gnyis pa yin/mkhar chen mgon mdo khams bum por sgang sde dge'i khongs rkam tshang gdung la rab byung bcu pa'i chu bya (1813)lor sku 'khrungs/chung dus g.yung drung nyi ma rgyal mtshan gyis rjes su bzung nas phyi nang gsang ba'i bon sde'i dbang lung phyag len dang rig gnas sogs la gsan sbyangs gnang/shes bya kun la lan re gcig pa tsam gyis gsal rtogs mkhyen pa'i spobs pa rgyas/de nas khu bo rin po che dang mnyam du gtsang la byon nas ba kra shis sman ri dgos par sgrigs zhugs mdzad/skyabs mchog zla ba rgyal mtshan sogs skyes chen du ma'i zhabs la gtugs nas mdo sngags rgyud sde'i tshig don kun la mkhas par sbyangs/khyad par du drang srong gi sdom khrims nyis brgya lnga bcu dang gsang sngags kyi sman ri'i srol 'dzin dgos lag mang po phyag btab/khong gi phyag rtsom la (shes rab kyi bla na med par phyin pa mngon rtogs rgyan gyi 'grel pa dga' gdan rab gsal )dang/(rnam bshad gzhung 'grel/) (rgyal ba mnyam med chen po'i rnam thar/) (bstod phyag sku gsung lhun grub kyi 'grel pa/) (thun mong sdud grwa'i rnam gzhag/) (rtsis gzhung 'od zer 'bum ldan/) (bstan rtsis nor bu'i phreng ba/) (brda dag 'bel gtam nyi 'od gzhon nu )sogs shin tu ming /de nas rje nyid mkhan zur du bzhugs rjes bsnyen sgrub mthar son te yi dam lha'i zhal gzigs par grags/zhal slob mchog tu gyur pa bsod nams phun tshogs dang/'gro mgon shes rab g.yung drung/mkhan chen skal bzang nyi ma sogs mang dag byung/dgongs rdzogs kyi sa tshod gnyis tu ma nges par bzhed pa mi 'dra ba cung zad yod rung/mang che bas rje 'di nyid They must have at least ten years of standing in the saṅgha, and their role is to confer ordination, to tend to the student, and to provide all the necessary requisites, therefore guiding that person for the taking of full vows and the maintenance of conduct and practice. This office was decreed by the Buddha so that aspirants would not have to receive ordination from the Buddha in person, and the Buddha identified two types: those who grant entry into the renunciate order and those who grant full ordination. The Tibetan translation mkhan po has also come to mean “a learned scholar,” the equivalent of apaṇḍita, but that is not the intended meaning in Indic Buddhist literature. 8drum, minstrel; kettle drum, drum, minstrel, gong9drum, minstrel36<term> paṇava37<term> paṇava (Skt: paṇava): Listed among Indian instruments as an hourglass drum, played in the hand, and the ancestor of the present day huḍukka, somewhat larger than the ḍamaru. See Saṅgītaśiromaṇi: A Medieval Handbook of Indian Music, edited by Emmie Te Nijenhuis, p. 549. However, Dutt describes it as a drum made of bell metal, which matches the Tibetan translation as “bronze drum,” but he may have been influenced by the Tibetan translation of chapter 30. In an earlier chapter paṇava is simply transcribed into Tibetan. An example of a bell metal drum would be the ceṇṇala, a small flat gong of bell metal that is hit with a stick and used to keep time in South Indian music. Other instruments mentioned are of the South Indian tradition. (Original glossary entry: https://84000.co/glossary/entity-3416.html)\n\nSynonym: {'khar ba'i rnga}38Synonym: {'khar ba'i rnga}39<term> paṇava421) paṇava 2) pāṇavika

par thugs rtse chen pos rjes su bzung ste/mdo sngags kyi chos rgyun spyi dang thun mong ma yin pa'i gdams pa yongs rdzogs cha tshang bar gnang zhing/de dag thugs dmas nyams bzhes su mdzad pa'i bogs 'don gyi ched du dam bca' bcu zhes pa mdzad('di zur du bton yod )nas de las nang yang 'da' ba med par sku tshe mtha' 'khyor ba gnang/dgung lo zhe gsum pa me lug (1427 lor rgyal rtse da srid rab brtan 2Khedrubje, a disciple of {rje btsong kha pa} Syn {mkhas grub dge legs dpal bzang}9R]R]R] - Khedrupje [disciple of Tshongkhapa = {mkhas grub dge legs dpal bzang}26{mkhas grub dge legs dpal bzang dang don gcig}36<person> Khedrub Jé, Khedrup Jé37<person> Khedrub Jé, Khedrup Jé: \n1) One of the principal pupils of Tsongkhapa, the founder of the Gelug tradition. Also retrospectively know as the first Panchen Lama (b. 1385−d. 1438).\n2) 1385–1438. A famous Tibetan scholar trained in the Sakya school and the Kadampa lineage. He later became one of the foremost disciples of Tsongkhapa (tsong kha pa; 1357–1419). He became the third throne holder of Ganden monastery after Tsongkhapa, and posthumously was declared the first Paṇchen Lama (paN chen bla ma).\n3) Khedrup Jé Gelek Palsang (mkhas grub rje dge legs dpal bzang, 1385–1438) one of the main disciples of Tsongkhapa.\n\n(Original glossary entry: https://84000.co/glossary/entity-36581.html)42paṇḍirasiddhasvāmin52(Skt. Khedrubje) Khedrubje53{mkhas grub dge legs dpal bzang gi mtshan gzhan zhig tshig 'di gsar du bcos pa yin/}55{mkhas grub dge legs dpal bzang dang don gcig}
2Khedrub Je, Gelek Palzang (1385-1438), one of Tsongkhapa' main disciples
0Khay-drub [1385-1438; one of Tsong-kha-pa's two main students and 3rd abbot of Gan-dan (dga' ldan) monastery]
